# Microbiology Question Bank

Converted from the non-mock question-bank JSON files in the Medical Companion repository. Correct options are marked **(Correct)**; explanations follow each option when available.

## Antimicrobials

Source: `{path.name}`

### Penicillins

1. How do penicillins kill bacteria?
   - **A.** They block folate synthesis — Wrong. Sulfonamides.
   - **B.** They disrupt the cell membrane — Wrong. Daptomycin.
   - **C.** They inhibit DNA gyrase — Wrong. Fluoroquinolones.
   - **D.** They bind the 30S ribosome — Wrong. Aminoglycosides.
   - **E.** As D-Ala-D-Ala analogs they bind transpeptidases, block peptidoglycan cross-linking and activate autolytic enzymes **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 183

2. Which form of penicillin is given orally?
   - **A.** Penicillin V **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Piperacillin — Wrong. Different drug.
   - **C.** Penicillin G — Wrong. IV and IM.
   - **D.** Both G and V equally — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Nafcillin — Wrong. Different drug.
   - Page: 183

3. Which organisms does penicillin G cover?
   - **A.** Mycoplasma — Wrong. No cell wall.
   - **B.** MRSA — Wrong. Not covered.
   - **C.** Anaerobes below the diaphragm — Wrong. Metronidazole.
   - **D.** Gram-positive organisms, gram-negative cocci such as N meningitidis, and spirochetes such as T pallidum **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Pseudomonas — Wrong. Piperacillin.
   - Page: 183

4. Which adverse effects are associated with penicillin?
   - **A.** Ototoxicity — Wrong. Aminoglycosides.
   - **B.** Gray baby syndrome — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **C.** Tendon rupture — Wrong. Fluoroquinolones.
   - **D.** Hypersensitivity reactions, direct Coombs-positive hemolytic anemia and interstitial nephritis **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Tooth discoloration — Wrong. Tetracyclines.
   - Page: 183

5. How do bacteria resist penicillin?
   - **A.** β-lactamase cleaves the β-lactam ring, or PBPs mutate **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Efflux pumps only — Wrong. Tetracyclines.
   - **C.** Drug acetylation — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **D.** Altered D-Ala-D-Ala — Wrong. Vancomycin.
   - **E.** Ribosomal methylation — Wrong. Macrolides.
   - Page: 183

6. Which drugs are aminopenicillins?
   - **A.** Cefazolin — Wrong. Cephalosporin.
   - **B.** Penicillin G and V — Wrong. Not aminopenicillins.
   - **C.** Ampicillin and amoxicillin **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** Nafcillin and oxacillin — Wrong. Penicillinase resistant.
   - **E.** Piperacillin — Wrong. Antipseudomonal.
   - Page: 184

7. Which organisms do ampicillin and amoxicillin cover?
   - **A.** Pseudomonas and anaerobes — Wrong. Piperacillin.
   - **B.** Mycoplasma — Wrong. Not covered.
   - **C.** H influenzae, H pylori, E coli, Enterococci, Listeria, Proteus mirabilis, Salmonella and Shigella **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** Rickettsia — Wrong. Doxycycline.
   - **E.** MRSA — Wrong. Not covered.
   - Page: 184

8. Which aminopenicillin has greater oral bioavailability?
   - **A.** Ampicillin — Wrong. Lower.
   - **B.** Piperacillin — Wrong. Not oral.
   - **C.** They are equal — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Amoxicillin **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Penicillin V — Wrong. Not an aminopenicillin.
   - Page: 184

9. Why are nafcillin, oxacillin and dicloxacillin resistant to penicillinase?
   - **A.** They have no β-lactam ring — Wrong. They do.
   - **B.** They are not β-lactams — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** They are given with clavulanate — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** They bind a different PBP — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** A bulky R group blocks β-lactamase from reaching the β-lactam ring **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 184

10. What is the main clinical use of nafcillin?
   - **A.** Pseudomonas — Wrong. Piperacillin.
   - **B.** S aureus, except MRSA **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** Enterococci — Wrong. Ampicillin.
   - **D.** Listeria — Wrong. Ampicillin.
   - **E.** Anaerobes — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 184

11. Why does MRSA resist nafcillin and oxacillin?
   - **A.** It pumps the drug out — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** It modifies D-Ala-D-Ala — Wrong. Vancomycin.
   - **C.** It has an altered penicillin-binding protein target **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** It makes more β-lactamase — Wrong. Not the mechanism.
   - **E.** It lacks a cell wall — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 184

12. Which penicillin covers Pseudomonas?
   - **A.** Dicloxacillin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Amoxicillin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Penicillin G — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Nafcillin — Wrong. S aureus.
   - **E.** Piperacillin **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 184

13. Why is piperacillin usually combined with tazobactam?
   - **A.** To widen gram-positive coverage — Wrong. Not the reason.
   - **B.** Piperacillin is penicillinase sensitive, so a β-lactamase inhibitor protects it **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** To reduce kidney toxicity — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** To cover MRSA — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** To allow oral dosing — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 184

14. Which β-lactamase inhibitors are used with penicillins?
   - **A.** Cilastatin and probenecid — Wrong. Not inhibitors of β-lactamase.
   - **B.** Sulfamethoxazole — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Trimethoprim — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Allopurinol — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Clavulanic acid, avibactam, sulbactam and tazobactam **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 185

15. What class of drug is penicillin a prototype of?
   - **A.** Aminoglycosides — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** β-lactams **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** Glycopeptides — Wrong. Vancomycin.
   - **D.** Macrolides — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Fluoroquinolones — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 183

16. Which adverse effect can follow ampicillin or amoxicillin use?
   - **A.** Aplastic anemia — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **B.** Tendon rupture — Wrong. Fluoroquinolones.
   - **C.** Gray baby syndrome — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **D.** Rash and pseudomembranous colitis **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Ototoxicity — Wrong. Aminoglycosides.
   - Page: 184

17. Penicillin is bactericidal against which groups?
   - **A.** Gram-positive cocci and rods, gram-negative cocci and spirochetes **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Anaerobes only — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Gram-negative rods only — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Atypicals — Wrong. No cell wall.
   - **E.** Fungi — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 183

18. Which drug treats Actinomyces infection?
   - **A.** Vancomycin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Metronidazole — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Penicillin **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** TMP-SMX — Wrong. Nocardia.
   - **E.** Gentamicin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 183

19. Why is amoxicillin often combined with clavulanic acid?
   - **A.** To cover MRSA — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** To protect the drug from destruction by β-lactamase **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** To improve absorption — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** To reduce rash — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** To add Pseudomonas coverage — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 184

20. Which penicillin is used for gram-negative rods and anaerobes as well as Pseudomonas?
   - **A.** Piperacillin **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Amoxicillin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Dicloxacillin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Nafcillin — Wrong. S aureus.
   - **E.** Penicillin G — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 184

### Cephalosporins, carbapenems, aztreonam and vancomycin

21. Which organisms are typically not covered by first- through fourth-generation cephalosporins?
   - **A.** E coli and Klebsiella — Wrong. Covered.
   - **B.** Listeria, atypicals such as Chlamydia and Mycoplasma, MRSA and Enterococci **(Correct)** — Correct. LAME.
   - **C.** Streptococci — Wrong. Covered.
   - **D.** H influenzae — Wrong. Covered.
   - **E.** Proteus mirabilis — Wrong. Covered.
   - Page: 185

22. Which cephalosporin is given before surgery to prevent S aureus wound infections?
   - **A.** Cefepime — Wrong. Fourth generation.
   - **B.** Cefoxitin — Wrong. Second generation.
   - **C.** Ceftriaxone — Wrong. Third generation.
   - **D.** Ceftaroline — Wrong. Fifth generation.
   - **E.** Cefazolin **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 185

23. Which organisms do first-generation cephalosporins cover?
   - **A.** Anaerobes only — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Enterococci — Wrong. Not covered.
   - **C.** Pseudomonas — Wrong. Ceftazidime and cefepime.
   - **D.** MRSA — Wrong. Ceftaroline.
   - **E.** Gram-positive cocci, Proteus mirabilis, E coli and Klebsiella pneumoniae **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 185

24. Which cephalosporin generation adds H influenzae, Enterobacter aerogenes, Neisseria and Serratia?
   - **A.** Second **(Correct)** — Correct. HENS PEcK.
   - **B.** Fourth — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** First — Wrong. PEcK only.
   - **D.** Third — Wrong. Serious gram-negative.
   - **E.** Fifth — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 185

25. Which third-generation cephalosporin is used for meningitis, gonorrhea and disseminated Lyme disease?
   - **A.** Cefazolin — Wrong. First generation.
   - **B.** Ceftazidime — Wrong. Pseudomonas.
   - **C.** Cefoxitin — Wrong. Second generation.
   - **D.** Ceftriaxone **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Cefepime — Wrong. Fourth generation.
   - Page: 185

26. Which third-generation cephalosporin covers Pseudomonas?
   - **A.** Ceftazidime **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Ceftriaxone — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Cefixime — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Cefpodoxime — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Cefuroxime — Wrong. Second generation.
   - Page: 185

27. Which cephalosporin is the only one that covers MRSA?
   - **A.** Ceftaroline **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Cefepime — Wrong. Fourth generation.
   - **C.** Ceftriaxone — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Ceftazidime — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Cefazolin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 185

28. Which organism does ceftaroline not cover?
   - **A.** MRSA — Wrong. Covered.
   - **B.** Enterococcus faecalis — Wrong. Covered.
   - **C.** Pseudomonas **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** Streptococci — Wrong. Covered.
   - **E.** E coli — Wrong. Covered.
   - Page: 185

29. Which adverse effects are associated with cephalosporins?
   - **A.** Tendon rupture — Wrong. Fluoroquinolones.
   - **B.** Red man syndrome — Wrong. Vancomycin.
   - **C.** Tooth discoloration — Wrong. Tetracyclines.
   - **D.** Gray baby syndrome — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **E.** Hypersensitivity, autoimmune hemolytic anemia, a disulfiram-like reaction and vitamin K deficiency **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 185

30. How do cephalosporins affect aminoglycoside toxicity?
   - **A.** They block absorption — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** They increase ototoxicity only — Wrong. Not stated.
   - **C.** They have no effect — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** They reduce it — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** They increase nephrotoxicity **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 185

31. Why is imipenem given with cilastatin?
   - **A.** Cilastatin is a β-lactamase inhibitor — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Cilastatin widens the spectrum — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Cilastatin improves absorption — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Cilastatin inhibits renal dehydropeptidase I and prevents inactivation of the drug in the tubules **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Cilastatin prevents seizures — Wrong. Not the mechanism.
   - Page: 186

32. Which carbapenem is not active against Pseudomonas?
   - **A.** Imipenem — Wrong. Active.
   - **B.** None cover Pseudomonas — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Ertapenem **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** Meropenem — Wrong. Active.
   - **E.** All cover Pseudomonas — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 186

33. Which carbapenem has a lower risk of seizures and is stable to dehydropeptidase I?
   - **A.** Aztreonam — Wrong. Monobactam.
   - **B.** Meropenem **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** Imipenem — Wrong. Needs cilastatin.
   - **D.** Ertapenem — Wrong. Not stated.
   - **E.** Cefepime — Wrong. Cephalosporin.
   - Page: 186

34. Which adverse effect limits carbapenem use at high plasma levels?
   - **A.** Aplastic anemia — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **B.** Tendon rupture — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Ototoxicity — Wrong. Aminoglycosides.
   - **D.** CNS toxicity with seizures **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Gray baby syndrome — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 186

35. Which organisms inactivate carbapenems?
   - **A.** MRSA through altered PBPs — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Enterococcus through D-Ala-D-Lac — Wrong. Vancomycin.
   - **C.** K pneumoniae, E coli and Klebsiella aerogenes, through carbapenemases **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** Mycoplasma — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Anaerobes — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 186

36. A patient with a severe penicillin allergy and renal insufficiency needs coverage for gram-negative rods. Which drug is appropriate?
   - **A.** Vancomycin — Wrong. Gram positive.
   - **B.** Aztreonam **(Correct)** — Correct. No cross-allergenicity.
   - **C.** Ampicillin — Wrong. Allergy.
   - **D.** Gentamicin — Wrong. Renal issue.
   - **E.** Ceftriaxone — Wrong. Not the best here.
   - Page: 186

37. Which penicillin-binding protein does aztreonam bind?
   - **A.** PBP3 **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** PBP1 — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** PBP4 — Wrong. Not stated.
   - **D.** PBP2a — Wrong. MRSA.
   - **E.** It binds no PBP — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 186

38. How does vancomycin work?
   - **A.** It inhibits DNA gyrase — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** It disrupts the cell membrane — Wrong. Daptomycin.
   - **C.** It binds the D-Ala-D-Ala portion of cell wall precursors and blocks peptidoglycan formation **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** It binds the 50S ribosome — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** It inhibits transpeptidase directly — Wrong. β-lactams.
   - Page: 186

39. How do Enterococci become resistant to vancomycin?
   - **A.** Efflux pumps — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Altered PBPs — Wrong. MRSA.
   - **C.** Ribosomal methylation — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Modification of D-Ala-D-Ala to D-Ala-D-Lac **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** β-lactamase production — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 186

40. A patient develops diffuse flushing during a vancomycin infusion. How can this be prevented?
   - **A.** Adding cilastatin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Pretreatment with antihistamines and a slower infusion rate **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** Stopping the drug permanently — Wrong. Not needed.
   - **D.** Giving the drug orally — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Adding a β-lactamase inhibitor — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 186

### Protein synthesis inhibitors

41. Which protein synthesis inhibitors bind the 30S subunit?
   - **A.** Aminoglycosides and tetracyclines **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Macrolides and clindamycin — Wrong. 50S.
   - **C.** Fluoroquinolones — Wrong. DNA gyrase.
   - **D.** Streptogramins — Wrong. 50S.
   - **E.** Chloramphenicol and linezolid — Wrong. 50S.
   - Page: 187

42. Which protein synthesis inhibitors are bactericidal rather than bacteriostatic?
   - **A.** Aminoglycosides **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Tetracyclines — Wrong. Bacteriostatic.
   - **C.** Clindamycin — Wrong. Bacteriostatic.
   - **D.** Chloramphenicol — Wrong. Bacteriostatic.
   - **E.** Macrolides — Wrong. Bacteriostatic.
   - Page: 187

43. Why do these drugs spare human cells?
   - **A.** They are broken down in the liver — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** They bind only DNA — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** They target the bacterial 70S ribosome, not the human 80S ribosome **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** They cannot enter human cells — Wrong. Not the reason.
   - **E.** Humans lack ribosomes — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 187

44. How do aminoglycosides work?
   - **A.** They block tRNA attachment at the A site — Wrong. Tetracyclines.
   - **B.** They block translocation at 50S — Wrong. Clindamycin.
   - **C.** They block peptidyltransferase — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **D.** They inhibit RNA polymerase — Wrong. Rifampin.
   - **E.** They irreversibly inhibit the initiation complex at 30S, cause mRNA misreading and block translocation **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 187

45. Why are aminoglycosides ineffective against anaerobes?
   - **A.** Anaerobes have thick capsules — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Uptake of the drug requires oxygen **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** Anaerobes pump the drug out — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Anaerobes lack ribosomes — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Anaerobes make β-lactamase — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 187

46. Which adverse effect of aminoglycosides is an absolute contraindication in myasthenia gravis?
   - **A.** Ototoxicity — Wrong. Not the contraindication.
   - **B.** Teratogenicity — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Nephrotoxicity — Wrong. Not the contraindication.
   - **D.** Neuromuscular blockade **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Rash — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 187

47. Which drug class worsens aminoglycoside ototoxicity?
   - **A.** Statins — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Beta-blockers — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Loop diuretics **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** PPIs — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Thiazides — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 187

48. How do bacteria resist aminoglycosides?
   - **A.** Efflux pumps — Wrong. Tetracyclines.
   - **B.** Ribosomal methylation — Wrong. Macrolides.
   - **C.** β-lactamase — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Altered D-Ala-D-Ala — Wrong. Vancomycin.
   - **E.** Transferase enzymes acetylate, phosphorylate or adenylate the drug **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 187

49. Which aminoglycoside is used for bowel surgery preparation?
   - **A.** Streptomycin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Gentamicin — Wrong. Not the classic.
   - **C.** Tobramycin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Amikacin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Neomycin **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 187

50. How do tetracyclines work?
   - **A.** They inhibit folate synthesis — Wrong. Sulfonamides.
   - **B.** They block peptidyltransferase — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **C.** They bind 50S and block translocation — Wrong. Clindamycin.
   - **D.** They bind 30S and prevent attachment of aminoacyl-tRNA **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** They inhibit the initiation complex irreversibly — Wrong. Aminoglycosides.
   - Page: 188

51. Why should tetracyclines not be taken with milk, antacids or iron?
   - **A.** They raise drug levels dangerously — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** They inactivate the drug in the blood — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** They cause nausea together — Wrong. Not the reason.
   - **D.** They cause kidney stones — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Divalent cations block absorption in the gut **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 188

52. Which tetracycline is safe in renal failure, and why?
   - **A.** Tetracycline; it is not absorbed — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Doxycycline; it is eliminated in feces **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** None are safe — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Tigecycline; it is given IV — Wrong. Not the reason.
   - **E.** Minocycline; it is dialyzed — Wrong. Not stated.
   - Page: 188

53. Why are tetracyclines effective against Rickettsia and Chlamydia?
   - **A.** They accumulate inside cells, where these organisms live **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** They cross the blood-brain barrier well — Wrong. Limited CNS penetration.
   - **C.** They resist β-lactamase — Wrong. Not relevant.
   - **D.** They are bactericidal — Wrong. Bacteriostatic.
   - **E.** They bind 50S — Wrong. 30S.
   - Page: 188

54. Which adverse effects are typical of tetracyclines?
   - **A.** GI upset, tooth discoloration, inhibition of bone growth in children and photosensitivity **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Red man syndrome — Wrong. Vancomycin.
   - **C.** Seizures — Wrong. Carbapenems.
   - **D.** Gray baby syndrome — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **E.** Ototoxicity — Wrong. Aminoglycosides.
   - Page: 188

55. How do bacteria resist tetracyclines?
   - **A.** Altered PBPs — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Carbapenemase — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Reduced uptake or increased efflux by plasmid-encoded pumps **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** Ribosomal methylation — Wrong. Macrolides.
   - **E.** Drug acetylation — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - Page: 188

56. Which tetracycline derivative covers multidrug-resistant organisms such as MRSA and VRE?
   - **A.** Amikacin — Wrong. Aminoglycoside.
   - **B.** Doxycycline — Wrong. Not VRE.
   - **C.** Tetracycline — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** Tigecycline **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Minocycline — Wrong. Not stated.
   - Page: 188

57. How does chloramphenicol work?
   - **A.** It inhibits DNA gyrase — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** It blocks peptidyltransferase at the 50S subunit **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** It inhibits RNA polymerase — Wrong. Rifampin.
   - **D.** It binds 30S — Wrong. 50S.
   - **E.** It blocks translocation at 50S — Wrong. Clindamycin.
   - Page: 188

58. Why does chloramphenicol cause gray baby syndrome in premature infants?
   - **A.** It crosses the blood-brain barrier — Wrong. Not the reason.
   - **B.** It blocks the respiratory burst — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** They lack liver UDP-glucuronosyltransferase **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **D.** It binds hemoglobin — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Their kidneys cannot excrete it — Wrong. Not the stated reason.
   - Page: 188

59. Which hematologic adverse effect of chloramphenicol is dose independent?
   - **A.** Hemolytic anemia — Wrong. Not stated.
   - **B.** Thrombocytosis — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Dose-dependent anemia — Wrong. Dose dependent.
   - **D.** Aplastic anemia **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Neutrophilia — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 188

60. Clindamycin treats anaerobic infections in which part of the body, compared with metronidazole?
   - **A.** The skin only — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Above the diaphragm, such as aspiration pneumonia and lung abscess **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** The CNS only — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** The urinary tract — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Below the diaphragm — Wrong. Metronidazole.

### Linezolid, macrolides, polymyxins and folate inhibitors

61. How does linezolid work?
   - **A.** It blocks translocation at 50S — Wrong. Macrolides.
   - **B.** It binds 30S and causes misreading — Wrong. Aminoglycosides.
   - **C.** It blocks peptidyltransferase — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **D.** It binds 23S rRNA of the 50S subunit and prevents formation of the initiation complex **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** It inhibits DNA gyrase — Wrong. Not correct.
   - Page: 189

62. Which organisms does linezolid cover?
   - **A.** Gram-negative rods only — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Pseudomonas — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Anaerobes below the diaphragm — Wrong. Metronidazole.
   - **D.** Gram-positive species including MRSA and VRE **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **E.** Atypicals — Wrong. Macrolides.
   - Page: 189

63. A patient on linezolid and an SSRI becomes agitated with tremor, fever and overactive reflexes. Why?
   - **A.** It is a hypersensitivity reaction — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** Linezolid is a partial MAO inhibitor, so serotonin syndrome can occur **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** Linezolid causes neuropathy — Wrong. Different effect.
   - **D.** The SSRI raises linezolid levels — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **E.** Linezolid blocks serotonin reuptake — Wrong. Not the mechanism.
   - Page: 189

64. Which hematologic adverse effect is typical of linezolid?
   - **A.** Megaloblastic anemia — Wrong. Trimethoprim.
   - **B.** Methemoglobinemia — Wrong. Dapsone.
   - **C.** Hemolysis in G6PD deficiency — Wrong. Sulfonamides and dapsone.
   - **D.** Aplastic anemia — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - **E.** Myelosuppression, especially thrombocytopenia **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - Page: 189

65. How do macrolides work?
   - **A.** They inhibit folate synthesis — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **B.** They bind the 50S subunit and block translocation **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **C.** They bind 30S — Wrong. 50S.
   - **D.** They prevent initiation complex formation — Wrong. Linezolid.
   - **E.** They block peptidyltransferase — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
   - Page: 189

66. Which infections are macrolides used for?
   - **A.** Atypical pneumonias, chlamydial STIs, streptococcal infections in penicillin-allergic patients, and B pertussis **(Correct)** — Correct.
   - **B.** Tuberculosis — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **C.** Pseudomonas — Wrong. Not correct.
   - **D.** MRSA and VRE — Wrong. Linezolid.
   - **E.** Anaerobes below the diaphragm — Wrong. Metronidazole.
   - Page: 189

67. Which adverse effects are associated with macrolides?
   - **A.** Gray baby syndrome — Wrong. Chloramphenicol.
